The same function serves account names and\n  category descriptions.\n- `validate_memo(value)` accepts 0 to 60 characters. The empty string is valid.\n- `validate_name` and `validate_memo` reject any value containing a comma or a newline.\n- An accepted value is returned unchanged. A rejected value raises `ValueError`. The command layer\n  maps that exception to exit 3 with no change to the database.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC code-accepts-bounds ===\nIntent: validate_code returns a one-character code validate_code(longest) == longest\n=== END AC code-accepts-bounds ===\n\n=== AC code-rejects-length ===\nIntent: validate_code rejects an empty code and a thirteen-character code.\n\nfrom validation import validate_code\n\n\ndef rejected(check, value):\n    try:\n        check(value)\n    except ValueError:\n        return True\n    return False\n\n\nempty = \"\"\ntoo_long = \"A\" * 13\nassert rejected(validate_code, empty) is True\nassert rejected(validate_code, too_long) is True\n=== END AC code-rejects-length ===\n\n=== AC code-rejects-characters ===\nIntent: validate_code rejects This module is the\nsingle parser for all of them. It lives at `dates.py` in the application root.\n\n## Rules\n\n- `parse_date(text)` accepts exactly the form `YYYY-MM-DD` with four digits, a two-digit month, and\n  a two-digit day.\n- The date must exist on the calendar. Leap years are honored, so `2028-02-29` is valid and\n  `2026-02-29` is not.\n- Impossible dates such as `2026-02-30`, month `13`, and day `00` are rejected.\n- Any other shape, including `2026-1-01`, `20260101`, and `2026/01/01`, is rejected.\n- Success returns a `datetime.date`. Rejection raises `ValueError`, which the command layer maps to\n  exit 3.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC date-round-trip ===\nIntent: Valid calendar dates parse to a datetime.date whose ISO form equals the input.\n\nimport datetime\nfrom dates import parse_date\n\nfor text in (\"2026-01-01\", \"2026-02-28\", \"2028-02-29\", \"2026-12-31\"):\n    result = parse_date(text)\n    assert isinstance(result, datetime.date)\n    assert result.isoformat() == text\n=== END AC date-round-trip ===\n\n=== AC date-rejects-impossible ===\nIntent: Dates that do not exist on the calendar are rejected.\n\nfrom dates import parse_date\n\n\ndef rejected(check, value):\n    try:\n        check(value)\n    except ValueError:\n        return True\n    return False\n\n\nfor text in (\"2026-02-30\", \"2026-02-29\", \"2026-04-31\", \"2026-13-01\", \"2026-00-10\", \"2026-01-00\"):\n    assert rejected(parse_date, text) is True\n=== END AC date-rejects-impossible ===\n\n=== AC date-rejects-malformed ===\nIntent: Text that is not in the YYYY-MM-DD shape is rejected.\n\nfrom dates import parse_date\n\n\ndef rejected(check, value):\n    try:\n        check(value)\n    except ValueError:\n        return True\n    return False\n\n\nfor text in (\"\", \"2026-1-01\", \"2026-01-1\", \"20260101\", \"26-01-01\", \"2026/01/01\", \"not-a-date\"):\n    assert rejected(parse_date, text) is True\n=== END AC date-rejects-malformed ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The parser never reads the system clock; = [\"DRAWER1\"]\n    before = len(db.transactions.list())\n    try:\n        check_non_negative(db, touched)\n    except ValueError:\n        raised = True\n    else:\n        raised = False\n    after = len(db.transactions.list())\nassert raised is True\nassert after == before\n=== END AC balance-rejects-negative ===\n\n=== AC balance-allows-zero ===\nIntent: A touched account whose balance = True\n    try:\n        check_non_negative(db, touched)\n    except ValueError:\n        ok = False\nassert ok is True\n=== END AC balance-allows-zero ===\n\n=== AC balance-checks-only-touched ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Amount-Format.md ===\n# FEATURE: Amount Format\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Parse input amounts into integer cents and format stored cents for output. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | parse_amount_cents, format_amount_cents |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery amount crossing the command boundary is converted here. The module lives at `amounts.py` in\nthe application root. No floating-point value is produced or accepted at any point.\n\n## Rules\n\n- `parse_amount_cents(text)` accepts text matching `-?DIGITS` optionally followed by `.D` or `.DD`.\n  It returns an `int` count of cents.\n- Input with a leading plus sign, surrounding whitespace, a thousands separator, a bare decimal\n  point, or no digits is rejected.\n- Zero in any form (`0`, `0.0`, `0.00`, `-0.00`) is rejected because an amount may not be zero.\n- `format_amount_cents(cents)` returns exactly two decimal places, a leading `-` when negative, and\n  no separators. A zero value is written `0.00`, never `-0.00`.\n- Rejections raise `ValueError`. The command layer maps them to exit 3.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC amount-round-trip ===\nIntent: Canonical amount strings parse to cents and format back to the same string.\n\nfrom amounts import parse_amount_cents, format_amount_cents\n\ncanonical = (\"12.50\", \"-12.50\", \"5000.00\", \"0.05\", \"-0.01\")\nfor text in canonical:\n    assert format_amount_cents(parse_amount_cents(text)) == text\n=== END AC amount-round-trip ===\n\n=== AC amount-short-forms-equal ===\nIntent: Amounts with one decimal or no decimal parse to the same cents as their two-decimal forms.\n\nfrom amounts import parse_amount_cents\n\nshort = \"12.5\"\nfull = \"12.50\"\nwhole = \"3\"\nwhole_full = \"3.00\"\nassert parse_amount_cents(short) == parse_amount_cents(full)\nassert parse_amount_cents(whole) == parse_amount_cents(whole_full)\n=== END AC amount-short-forms-equal ===\n\n=== AC amount-integer-cents ===\nIntent: parse_amount_cents returns a Python int, never a float.\n\nfrom amounts import parse_amount_cents\n\nsample = \"12.50\"\ncents = parse_amount_cents(sample)\nassert type(cents) is int\n=== END AC amount-integer-cents ===\n\n=== AC amount-rejects-zero ===\nIntent: Every zero form of an input amount is rejected.\n\nfrom amounts import parse_amount_cents\n\n\ndef rejected(check, value):\n    try:\n        check(value)\n    except ValueError:\n        return True\n    return False\n\n\nfor text in (\"0\", \"0.0\", \"0.00\", \"-0.00\"):\n    assert rejected(parse_amount_cents, text) is True\n=== END AC amount-rejects-zero ===\n\n=== AC amount-rejects-malformed ===\nIntent: Input that does not match the amount pattern is rejected.\n\nfrom amounts import parse_amount_cents\n\n\ndef rejected(check, value):\n    try:\n        check(value)\n    except ValueError:\n        return True\n    return False\n\n\nmalformed = (\"\", \"-\", \"abc\", \"1.234\", \"1,000.00\", \" 1.00\", \"+1.00\", \".50\", \"1.\", \"--1.00\", \"1.2.3\")\nfor text in malformed:\n    assert rejected(parse_amount_cents, text) is True\n=== END AC amount-rejects-malformed ===\n\n=== AC amount-format-zero-and-decimals ===\nIntent: Formatted amounts have exactly two decimals, a leading minus only when negative, and no separators.\n\nfrom amounts import format_amount_cents\n\nzero = 0\ntext = format_amount_cents(zero)\nassert text[0] != \"-\"\nassert len(text.split(\".\")[1]) == 2\n\nnegative = format_amount_cents(-5)\nassert negative[0] == \"-\"\nassert len(negative.split(\".\")[1]) == 2\n\nlarge = format_amount_cents(500000000)\nassert \",\" not in large\n=== END AC amount-format-zero-and-decimals ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- No floating-point arithmetic is used for money at any layer of this module.\n- The module never opens or reads the database.\n- Amount text is ASCII only.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Date-Validation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Date Validation\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Accept only real YYYY-MM-DD calendar dates for every command that takes a date. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | parse_date |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nDates enter the ledger through `txn post`, `transfer`, and `load transactions`. This module is the\nsingle parser for all of them. It lives at `dates.py` in the application root.\n\n## Rules\n\n- `parse_date(text)` accepts exactly the form `YYYY-MM-DD` with four digits, a two-digit month, and\n  a two-digit day.\n- The date must exist on the calendar. Leap years are honored, so `2028-02-29` is valid and\n  `2026-02-29` is not.\n- Impossible dates such as `2026-02-30`, month `13`, and day `00` are rejected.\n- Any other shape, including `2026-1-01`, `20260101`, and `2026/01/01`, is rejected.\n- Success returns a `datetime.date`. Rejection raises `ValueError`, which the command layer maps to\n  exit 3.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC date-round-trip ===\nIntent: Valid calendar dates parse to a datetime.date whose ISO form equals the input.\n\nimport datetime\nfrom dates import parse_date\n\nfor text in (\"2026-01-01\", \"2026-02-28\", \"2028-02-29\", \"2026-12-31\"):\n    result = parse_date(text)\n    assert isinstance(result, datetime.date)\n    assert result.isoformat() == text\n=== END AC date-round-trip ===\n\n=== AC date-rejects-impossible ===\nIntent: Dates that do not exist on the calendar are rejected.\n\nfrom dates import parse_date\n\n\ndef rejected(check, value):\n    try:\n        check(value)\n    except ValueError:\n        return True\n    return False\n\n\nfor text in (\"2026-02-30\", \"2026-02-29\", \"2026-04-31\", \"2026-13-01\", \"2026-00-10\", \"2026-01-00\"):\n    assert rejected(parse_date, text) is True\n=== END AC date-rejects-impossible ===\n\n=== AC date-rejects-malformed ===\nIntent: Text that is not in the YYYY-MM-DD shape is rejected.\n\nfrom dates import parse_date\n\n\ndef rejected(check, value):\n    try:\n        check(value)\n    except ValueError:\n        return True\n    return False\n\n\nfor text in (\"\", \"2026-1-01\", \"2026-01-1\", \"20260101\", \"26-01-01\", \"2026/01/01\", \"not-a-date\"):\n    assert rejected(parse_date, text) is True\n=== END AC date-rejects-malformed ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The parser never reads the system clock; it validates only the text it is given.\n- The module never opens or reads the database.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CLI-Help.md ===\n# FEATURE: CLI Help\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Print one usage line per command in the stderr usage diagnostic when ledger is run without a valid command. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CLI-Dispatch.md |\n| Provides    | usage listing on stderr, cli.usage.usage_lines |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nWhen `ledger` is run with no arguments, or with a command that lacks its required sub-verb or\narguments, the usage diagnostic lists every command in the Commands table. cli_runner\nfrom cli.usage import usage_lines\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner()\nlines = usage_lines()\nassert err.splitlines() == lines\n=== END AC help-stderr-matches-usage-lines ===\n\n=== AC FEATURE-Balance-Rule.md ===\n# FEATURE: Balance Rule\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Reject any command whose changes leave a touched account with a negative balance, before the transaction commits. |\n| Depends On  | DATABASE.md, FEATURE-Command-Transaction.md, FEATURE-Amount-Format.md |\n| Provides    | check_non_negative |\n| Consumes    | transactions.list |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nPosting, amending, deleting, transferring, and loading all end with this check. The check runs inside\nthe command's transaction after its changes and before commit. The module lives at `balance.py` in\nthe application root.\n\n## Rules\n\n- `check_non_negative(db, accounts)` takes the open `Database` and a list of the account codes that\n  the command touched.\n- For each listed account, the balance is the sum of the `amount` values of that account's\n  transactions, in integer cents, computed from the rows returned by `transactions.list`.\n- Any listed account whose balance is below zero causes `ValueError` to be raised.\n- A balance of exactly zero is allowed.\n- Accounts not in the list are not checked, even when their balance is negative.\n- An empty list is accepted and performs no rejection.\n- The check only reads. It never changes a row. The command layer maps the raised `ValueError` to\n  exit 3 and rolls back the transaction.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC balance-rejects-negative ===\nIntent: A touched account whose balance falls below zero raises ValueError and leaves every row unchanged.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom balance import check_non_negative\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.categories.create(category=\"SALES\", description=\"Sales\")\n    db.accounts.create(account=\"DRAWER1\", name=\"Front Drawer\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=1, date=\"2026-01-01\", account=\"DRAWER1\", category=\"SALES\", amount=500, memo=\"\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=2, date=\"2026-01-02\", account=\"DRAWER1\", category=\"SALES\", amount=-600, memo=\"\")\n    touched = [\"DRAWER1\"]\n    before = len(db.transactions.list())\n    try:\n        check_non_negative(db, touched)\n    except ValueError:\n        raised = True\n    else:\n        raised = False\n    after = len(db.transactions.list())\nassert raised is True\nassert after == before\n=== END AC balance-rejects-negative ===\n\n=== AC balance-allows-zero ===\nIntent: A touched account whose balance is exactly zero does not raise.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom balance import check_non_negative\n\nopening = 500\nspend = -opening\ntouched = [\"DRAWER1\"]\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.categories.create(category=\"SALES\", description=\"Sales\")\n    db.accounts.create(account=\"DRAWER1\", name=\"Front Drawer\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=1, date=\"2026-01-01\", account=\"DRAWER1\", category=\"SALES\", amount=opening, memo=\"\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=2, date=\"2026-01-02\", account=\"DRAWER1\", category=\"SALES\", amount=spend, memo=\"\")\n    ok = True\n    try:\n        check_non_negative(db, touched)\n    except ValueError:\n        ok = False\nassert ok is True\n=== END AC balance-allows-zero ===\n\n=== AC balance-checks-only-touched ===\nIntent: A negative account that is not in the touched list is ignored, and the same account raises when it is touched.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom balance import check_non_negative\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    db.categories.create(category=\"SALES\", description=\"Sales\")\n    db.accounts.create(account=\"DRAWER1\", name=\"Front Drawer\")\n    db.accounts.create(account=\"OTHER\", name=\"Other Account\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=1, date=\"2026-01-01\", account=\"DRAWER1\", category=\"SALES\", amount=500, memo=\"\")\n    db.transactions.create(txn_id=2, date=\"2026-01-02\", account=\"OTHER\", category=\"SALES\", amount=-100, memo=\"\")\n    untouched_ok = True\n    try:\n        check_non_negative(db, [\"DRAWER1\"])\n    except ValueError:\n        untouched_ok = False\n    touched_raised = False\n    try:\n        check_non_negative(db, [\"OTHER\"])\n    except ValueError:\n        touched_raised = True\nassert untouched_ok is True\nassert touched_raised is True\n=== END AC balance-checks-only-touched ===\n\n=== AC balance-empty-list-accepts ===\nIntent: An empty touched list performs no rejection.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom balance import check_non_negative\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    ok = True\n    try:\n        check_non_negative(db, [])\n    except ValueError:\n        ok = False\nassert ok is True\n=== END AC balance-empty-list-accepts ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The balance check reads through the `persistence` package only and never imports `sqlite3` or a\n  database driver.\n- Balances are computed in integer cents with no floating-point arithmetic.\n- The check never writes to the database.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT
